REMARKs.-During April the highest reading of the barometer was 30.190, On the 9th; and the lowest 29.864, on the 7th. The highest temperature was 890, on the 3oth; the lowest 530, on the 9th; and the mean during month was 73*.8. S.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 8th, averaging 12.2 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 13 days, measuring 6.5 inches. There was a heavy fall of hail at 2.20 A.m. on the 9th. During May the highest reading of the barometer was 30.170, on the 3rd; and the lowest 29.786, on the 13th. The highest temperature was 94&#39;, on the i6th; the lowest 66&#39;, on the 26th; and the mean during month was si. S.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 31st, averaging 34 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 17 days, measuring 7.68 inches. During June the highest reading of the barometer was 3o.o60, on the 16th; and the lowest 29.700, on the 29th. The highest temperature was 970, on the 29th and 3oth; the lowest 730, on the 6th; and the mean during month was 80o.g. S.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 24th, averaging 30.2 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 23 days, measuring 15.64 inches. IX. PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended June 30, 1894.
166 CUSTOX8 GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 284. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. MIDDLE ISLAND SURVEYING BEACON. NOTICE is hereby given that, on account of the washing away of the eastern shore of Middle Island, the Surveying Beacon, No. 45 in the published List for 1894, has been shifted 6 cables S. 840 W., magnetic, from its former position. SHANGHAI, 4th June 1894. No. 285. YANGTZE RIVER-KIUKI.ANG DISTRICT. KIUKIANG ROCKS LIGHT-BOAT ESTABLISHED; KIUKIANG ROCKS LIGHT AND KIUKIANG ROCKS BEACON LIGHT DISCONTINUED. NOTICE is hereby given that a light-boat has been moored abreast of Kiukiang Rocks and close to the upper end of Oliphant Island, where it marks the starboard side of the channel for vessels entering from seaward. The illuminating apparatus is fixed, dioptric, of the sixth order, showing a red light, which should be visible in clear weather at a distance of 4 nautical miles. The light-boat is painted red, and its mast is surmounted by a black ball. The Kiukiang Rocks Light and the Kiukiang Rocks Beacon Light, Nos. 86 and 87 respectively in the published List for 1894, have been discontinued. SHANGHAI, 5th June 1894.
1894.] NOTIFICATIONS. 167 No. 286. YANGTZE RIVER-CHINKIANG DISTRICT. NORTH TREE BEACON LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the North Tree Beacon Light has been shifted 200 yards N. So&#39; E. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the river bank. SHANGHAI, 6th June 1894. No. 287. KIUNGCHOW DISTRICT. HOIHOW HARBOUR LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the Hoihow Harbour Light was exhibited for the first time at sunset on the ISth instant. The illuminating apparatus is a four-sided dioptric one of the sixth order, showing three WHITE flashes in rapid succession, followed by a 8ingle RED flash every 45 seconds. The alternation of flash and eclipse in each revolution is, approximately- White flash . . . .. . . . . . . . . . . I seconds. Eclipse ....... .............. 6 White flash .. .......... ............ , Eclipse . ............. ...... 6 White flash................... ,, Eclipse .......... .......... 131 Red flash ...... . , Eclipse ....... .............. 131 45 The lighthouse stands 464 yards south of high-water mark on the southern shore of Hoihow Bay, and the light, which is elevated 73 feet above the level of the sea, should be visible in clear weather at a distance of io miles in all directions where it is not obscured by land. The tower is round, of iron, with a total height from its base to lantern vane of 22 feet. The tower, dwellings, and boundary wall are white. Approximate position:- Latitude . . . ... . . . . . . 200 1&#39; 15&quot; N. Longitude . . . . . . . . ... . I io 16&#39; io&quot; E. SHANGHAI, 26th June 1894.

194 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, (Beceived 14th July 1894-) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 10TH JULY 1894. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publicatiop, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the second quarter of the year 1894, April-June. b. The Custom House (:L L ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Conveition Likin on Opium (Hk.A 3o and Hk.T 8o per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon ( Pj * ), Changehow (f f), Fotochow ( Xf 09), and Kowloon (* #1) stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. 1-894.] LAPPA. 205 (Received 16th July 1894.) CUSTOM. HOUSE, LAPPA, 10TH. JULY I894. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second &amp;amp;eries, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the second quarter of the year 1894, April-June. b. The Custom House ( t a) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.ft 3o and Hk. 8o per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow ( R &#39; ) and Chienshan (* J) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (W A), Kuttai (-- L ) and Kwanchiap ( () sub-stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II. 1894.] YATUNG. 221 (Received 31st JTliY 1894.) CUSTOM HOUSE, YATUNG, 2ND JULY 1894. SIR, .-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your CirculArs Nos. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and 6 of 1875, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Yatung for the second quarter of the year 1894. b. The Yatung Custom House was opened on the Ist May 1894.. II.-REPORT&sect; TO THE CUSTOMS.-No statistics recorded. (On the average porters carry 6o catties and mules I20 catties per head.) III. 222 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, 1894.1 IV. EXPORTS.-The principal articles of Export, during the quarter, have been as follows:- DESCRIPTION oF GooDs. 1894I DEsRIPTION or GoODS. 1894. Maunds 5,430 Horns, Deer............. ..........,, V. RE-EXPORTS.-Nil. VI. INTERNAL TRANSIT.-Nil. VII. OPIUM.-Nil. VIII. SPECIAL TABLES.-Treasure: Rs. 20,765 imported; Rs. 4oo exported. IX. REVENUE.-None collectible for five years. X. GENERAL REMARKS.-The principal items amongst the Imports are Cotton Piece Goods, Maize, Copperware, Ironware, Tobacco, and Woollen Cloth. In regard to the Cotton Piece Goods, it is as well to explain that they consist almost entirely of a very flimsy material, which is used for decorating places or objects of a religious character. The white cloth is printed with invocations and attached lengthwise down poles, which are planted about villages and at the approaches to temples. The coloured cloths are used as streamers and in ornamenting tombs, etc.; the colours are blue, green, yellow, and red. The price is extremely low. . . . . . . . While the value of the goods imported amounted to Us. 54,508, Us. 20,765 were also imported in coin, which makes the total-viz., Rs. 75,273 -very nearly balance the total value of the Exports-viz., Rs. 78,781. The only item of importance amongst the Exports is Sheep&#39;s Wool, which is valued at Rs. 76,020; but I believe that during the autumn and winter Musk and other articles will also take an important place. The trade is likely to fall off during the September quarter, owing to the heavy rains in Sikkim. All goods are carried by men or on pack-animals. A man carries i maund, and a mule 2 maunds. The cost of carriage to Kalimpong, which is the principal market and which is about 24 miles from Darjeeling, is about Rs. 3 per maund. A maund weighs 8o lb., or 6o catties, and is divided into 40 seers; a seer contains 8o tolas. I am, etc., F. E. TAYLOR, Commissioner 6f Customs. To the INSPECTOR GENERAL OF CUSTOMS.
